| verified | FCC 26-19 (Network and Services Modernization Order, adopted Mar 26 2026) rules for copper retirement and discontinuance became effective Oct 15 2026. The FCC no longer files or issues public notices for copper retirements or short-term network changes, and the objection process for interconnected providers is gone. Notice must be publicly accessible, for example on the ILEC's website. Direct notice now also goes to 911 service providers. Direct notice is 90 days before a copper retirement, or 15 days if the copper serves no customers. <br>*"the Commission: (1) eliminated all filing requirements in the Commission's network change disclosure rules and the Commission's process of issuing public notices for short-term network changes and copper retirements and the associated objection process for interconnected service providers"* | 2026-10-01 (FR); effecti | https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-10-01/pdf/2026-20192.pdf |

**Idea seeds:**
- ECRC Zero: Verizon must report how many MLTS 911 calls hit Intrado's national relay center (DA 26-1042; first report 60 days after Sept 28). A campus or enterprise tool could verify that every PBX extension's 911 call reaches the right PSAP with dispatchable location before NJ's NG911 Phase 1. The PS/ALI-to-Verizon template migration is a named Verizon program.
- Outage Credit Autopilot: NJ A5548 (Sept 24 2026) would require automatic 1/30-of-monthly-bill credits per 3 hours of complete outage, with $1,000-per-customer penalties. An AI system could compute per-customer outage credits from network telemetry and turn noisy outage alarms into the 'actionable' 30-minute PSAP and 988 notices DA 26-1033 demands. Pillar 1/2; Verizon-internal.
- A Rutgers comment in FCC PS Docket 26-197 (due Nov 16 2026) on IoT and life-safety endpoints in the 911 framework: a credible primary-source artifact for the poster.
- Nov 17 2026, 2:20 pm ET nationwide EAS test (DA 26-989): a fixed natural experiment for any campus alert-reach measurement before the Feb 3 poster. It is EAS only, not WEA.
- The WEA FNPRM proposes ending the 'location services off' geotargeting exception and adding a universal alert ID. A campus could measure WEA overshoot and duplicates now to give Verizon a pre-compliance baseline. Close to explored Alert Clock/AlertCell ideas, so use caution.
- NJ S4782/A5661 would impose an all-elevators retrofit (standard fire service keys) as a condition of inspection renewal. This shows NJ will impose retroactive elevator mandates, a policy path for an 'every car proves HELP works' rule.
- FCC robocall scorecard (DA 26-932): the FCC will publicly rate providers. Verizon has a reputational stake in measured smish and robocall blocking on campus. HangUp/RU Real are adjacent, so differentiate.
- The COSMOS (NYC) Innovation Zone was renewed for 5 years (DA 26-911, Aug 28 2026). Experimental licensing for a Rutgers-linked testbed, usable for any prototype needing spectrum.
- FCC 26-66 UWB NPRM proposes a new UWB category for AI-enabled sensing (Sept 30 2026). Seed for camera-free indoor sensing, though occupancy/Circadian ideas were killed.
- NJ P.L.2026 c.25 bans data brokers from selling precise geolocation (1,750 ft). Pair it with the $46.9M FCC fine: a 'location never leaves the network' design is now a NJ-law talking point.

## 16. Verizon cost lines a campus pilot could move (`sweep_verizon_costs`, confidence medium)

Verizon does not disclose care call volume, cost per contact, truck-roll counts or copper O&M; those stay UNVERIFIED. Verified from filings and transcripts: (1) CHURN is the biggest lever. Total postpaid phone churn was 0.98% in FY2025 (0.88% in 2024), 1.02% in Q4'25, 0.97% in Q1'26 and 0.92% in Q2'26 (consumer 0.84%). Schulman, Jan 30 2026: 'every single basis point is 90,000 net adds', and he names friction in onboarding, billing and customer service calls as one of four churn causes. (2) RETENTION/SUBSIDY: equipment sold at about -$3.5B in FY2025 ($25.47B revenue vs $28.98B cost). In Q2'26 CoA fell about 15% and CoR about 17%, and upgrades fell about 27%. Schulman: a $1,000 free handset for a home-coverage complaint where a femtocell costs one-third. (3) BAD DEBT: FY2025 credit-loss provisions were $2.11B and write-offs $1.90B. Customers under 210 days hold about 18% of device receivables but caused about 65% of device write-offs. (4) FRAUD: FCC DA 26-43 says 784,703 devices were lost to fraud in 2023, 'hundreds of millions' a year. (5) COPPER: about $180M a year saved from 4.5M circuits and 36 COs (2022). Emptied COs are being turned into data centers (Jul 2026). (6) RETAIL: 274 stores went to franchisees on Aug 16 2026, leaving about 1,000. New: FCC DA 26-1038 (Sept 25 2026) pulled Verizon's 9-state legacy-voice discontinuance, NJ included, out of auto-grant. Q3'26 results come Oct 26. Student-data targets: retention spend misdirected to handsets, new-customer bad debt and fraud, and copper-retirement friction.

**Implication for Last Copper.** Mixed, net slightly stronger if reframed, weaker as written. WEAKER: the 'cut on or after Dec 10, 2026' countdown is now shaky. FCC DA 26-1038 (Sept 25, 2026) removed Verizon's section 214 application covering 'portions of New Jersey' (WC Docket 26-227) from automatic grant. The NJ copper notice itself says Verizon will discontinue copper services 'pursuant to FCC rules before retiring the facilities', so the Dec 10 date may slip. Never say 'the copper dies Dec 10' on video without a caveat. UNVERIFIED: whether Piscataway (PSWYNJPI) is inside the 26-227 'portions of NJ' (ECFS returned 403). STRONGER: the bottleneck has moved from finding lines to proving that replacements work for vulnerable and emergency users. That is the objection Virginia counties (Accomack, Augusta, Rappahannock) and Rep. Suhas Subramanyam filed in 26-227; NJ county objections are reported only in trade press (unverified). PROVE's nightly evidence and Cutover Watch can be pitched as the record that helps Verizon win discontinuance approval faster. Copper retirement pays: about $180M a year from 36 COs (2022), about 60% fewer dispatches, and emptied COs now become data centers that 'sold out capability in 24 hours'. Each month of delay costs Verizon, and Last Copper now attacks the delay rather than the deadline. Do not credit copper savings to the team; cite them only as why Verizon wants retirement to go fast. Rewrite the hook from 'countdown to Dec 10' to 'the FCC paused Verizon's NJ copper exit until it is sure people can still call for help. We prove they can, every night.'

**Idea seeds:**
- Fix the place, not the phone: Schulman's own Apr 27 2026 anecdote ($1,000 free handset vs a femtocell at one-third the cost) plus CoR down 17%. Student-collected, consented indoor experience data by building (dorms, off-campus blocks) could route save-offers to place fixes (Network Extender, FWA, Wi-Fi Calling setup) instead of handsets. The cost line is retention spend, not coverage. Risk: close to explored 'Glass Ceiling'.
- Churn math on campus: 1 bps of churn = 90,000 net adds (Jan 30 2026). Students hit churn moments every year (arrival, move-out, graduation off the family plan, international arrival). A student-run panel survey on switching intent and its triggers is primary data only Rutgers students can collect, uses no CPNI, and maps to Schulman's 'four reasons people leave'.
- New-customer bad debt: tenure under 210 days = about 65% of FY2025 device write-offs ($884M of $1,356M) on about 18% of receivables. Each 0.25% loss-rate change = $167M. A consented university-enrollment attestation (a positive signal, not CPNI) for first-own-line students could be tested as a lower-risk new-customer segment. Risk: overlaps explored 'First Number' and 'Day-Zero Line'.
- Device trafficking on campus: FCC DA 26-43 (Jan 12 2026) cites 784,703 devices lost to fraud in 2023, 'hundreds of millions' a year, synthetic-identity credit fraud, a resale premium of $300 or more, and 46 robberies at DC-area stores. Students could measure campus-adjacent resale and recruitment ('sell your new iPhone') ads. Legal and safety review needed. UNVERIFIED as a Rutgers problem.
- Copper Out, Compute In: Schulman (Jul 24 2026) says Verizon has 'thousands of central offices' it is emptying of copper and retrofitting as power-ready remote data centers, and a trial 'sold out capability in 24 hours'. The Piscataway CO (4 Skiles Ave) sits next to Busch and Livingston. Rutgers research compute or edge-AI demand could anchor an AI Connect edge site. This ties copper retirement to new revenue. Rutgers demand is UNVERIFIED.
- Docket miner: WC Docket 26-227 holds hundreds of public comments, plus counties (Accomack, Augusta, Rappahannock VA), Rep. Suhas Subramanyam and Verizon's Sept 24 reply and Oct 9 supplemental letter. An AI taxonomy of objections tied to mitigation evidence could speed Verizon's discontinuance approvals. The data is live and dated after mid-2026, and it is Verizon-specific. Pillar 1.
- Store-to-franchise transition: 274 stores moved to franchisees Aug 16 2026, about 1,000 company stores remain, and 179 were sold in Nov 2025. Student mystery-shopper data comparing service on campus-area franchise vs corporate stores, on retention-critical journeys, is primary data. Value to Verizon is UNVERIFIED.

## 17. Students, carriers and churn (`sweep_students_churn`, confidence medium)

VERIFIED (primary): Verizon postpaid phones turned positive in 2026. Q1 added 55k (consumer churn 0.90%). Q2 added 184k (Q2 2025: -9k; H1 2026: 239k vs -298k), with total churn 0.92% and consumer churn 0.84%. Postpaid accounts were still down 1.2% YoY and ARPA down 1.4%. Core prepaid churns 3.59% a month, about 4x postpaid. Guidance is the upper half of 750k-1.0M, "2 to 3 times" 2025. On the Q2 call Schulman cited "a lot more of the youth market" but gave no number. Q3 results come out Oct 26, 2026, after today. Youth offers in 2026: the student discount ($10 for 1 line or $25 for 2) requires the student to be the Account Owner or Manager, so students on a parent plan cannot get it. Simplicity costs $45 ($30 for switchers). Visible offers graduates $20/mo or a $275 year parents can gift. T-Mobile student plans start at $30 for a primary holder 18 or older. VERIFIED (vendor or older surveys): Gen Z leaves family plans at 19-22 and 45% want their own plan (OXIO 2026). 58% of Gen Z would switch carriers but a family plan holds them (KPMG 2024). New international enrollment fell 17% in fall 2025; NAFSA projects up to 111k fewer students in fall 2026. Rutgers has about 8,000 international students and OPT holders. UNVERIFIED: any churn rate at graduation, carrier or prepaid share among 18-24-year-olds, international students' carrier choice, AT&T/T-Mobile Q2 numbers. The session web-search budget ran out before these could be followed up.

**Implication for Last Copper.** Neutral to slightly supportive, and nothing here kills it. This sweep is about consumer phone churn, while Last Copper is a business and life-safety migration play, so the student data neither strengthens nor weakens its core. One thing does carry over: Verizon management now says openly that churn is its main lever. Schulman: 'at least half, if not more than half, of our net adds are going to come from improvements in churn.' That makes the 'retention at a forced-migration moment' framing land with Verizon judges, provided Last Copper says it is retaining E&PS and wireline relationships, not consumer phones. The sweep also warns against pivoting to a student-churn idea. Student and youth carrier ideas are the most crowded space (ScarletAgent, Day-Zero Line, Number for Life and First Number are already on our avoid list). T-Mobile (Jul 31, 2026) and Visible (May 18, 2026) are already attacking the graduation moment with offers. Any 'predict who is about to leave the family plan' feature would need subscriber data, which is CPNI-radioactive after the upheld $46.9M FCC fine. Keep Last Copper. If a consumer pillar is wanted, use it only as a footnote.

**Idea seeds:**
- Structural gap at the family-plan exit: Verizon's student discount requires the student to be the Account Owner or Manager, and Transfer of Service requires a soft credit check and possibly a deposit. Yet most 18-24-year-olds (49% in 2019) are on a parent's account, and OXIO says Gen Z leaves family plans at 19-22. Possible pitch: a Rutgers-verified 'graduation handoff', where the registrar or ID.me confirms graduation and the line moves from the parent's account to the graduate's own Simplicity line with no deposit and the same number. This is retention at the exact churn moment, aimed at Schulman's 'accounts not lines' and 'youth market' remarks. Check overlap with First Number and Number for Life (international-focused) before using.
- Primary data only Rutgers students can collect: a Class of 2027 senior census asking whose account your line is on, which carrier, whether you know about the student discount (which you cannot get on a parent's plan), and your plan after graduation. No public 2025-26 dataset gives carrier or plan status for 18-24-year-olds; that gap is the uniqueness.
- Verizon sends graduates to Visible, which is prepaid: core prepaid churn is 3.59% a month against 0.92% postpaid, and prepaid phone net adds fell 29.9% in H1 2026. A grad-cohort tracking study could test whether the Visible on-ramp leaks graduates to T-Mobile's $30 student or primary-holder plan with its 5-year price guarantee.
- International angle, though it may be shrinking and is close to Day-Zero Line: Rutgers has about 8,000 international students and OPT holders, and Rutgers-NB enrollment grew 8.5% in 2025 against a 17% national drop in new enrollment. The fall 2025 orientation release lists bank accounts and SSNs but no carrier or SIM step. The OPT population (1,852) is the post-graduation churn moment for international graduates.
- Timing hook: Verizon's Q3 2026 results come out Oct 26, 2026, before the Nov 8 video deadline. Any youth or account-growth comment there would be a live fact other teams' LLMs will not have.
- eSIM makes switching easier, which raises the stakes of the graduation moment: OXIO found 69% of Gen Z more likely to switch with instant eSIM activation, and 60% of Gen Z have used eSIM. A 'graduation-day eSIM handoff' would fit Verizon's push for digital self-service.

## 18. What is new in campus technology in 2026 (`sweep_campus_tech_2026`, confidence medium)

Method: WebSearch was exhausted (shared 200-call cap) before this agent ran, so zero searches were possible. Everything was checked by fetching primary sources directly: Federal Register API and PDFs, eCFR, govinfo, FBI IC3, FTC, Verizon, Skylo, NTIA and Rutgers OIT. Biggest LLM-default error: the ADA Title II web deadline is no longer April 2026. A DOJ interim final rule (Apr 20 2026) moved it to Apr 26 2027 for large public entities, and DOJ may reopen the WCAG 2.1 substance. Other verified 2026 changes: Title IX 2024 rule formally repealed (Sep 29); F/J students lost 'duration of status' (fixed admission of up to 4 years, effective Sep 15); CMMC Phase 2 about Nov 10 2026; CIRCIA still not final. FCC: copper and discontinuance streamlining took effect Oct 15 2026 (FCC 26-19, which rejected alarm-compatibility safeguards); new NG911 reliability, EAS cybersecurity, upper C-band auction (Apr 27 2027), 6 GHz GVP and unlicensed D2D NPRM rules; E-Rate hotspots rescinded. Threats: FBI PSAs on ShinyHunters hitting an LMS, swatting shifting to schools, Kali365 device-code MFA bypass, and impersonation scams naming international students ($1.6B). Hardware: AT&T/T-Mobile/Verizon D2D JV (Oct 1), Skylo-certified LTE-M+satellite modules, eSIM-only foldable iPhone. Rutgers: RUWireless turned off Jan 2027. Unverified: which LMS was hit, RedCap device counts, Verizon satellite texting in 2026, EDUCAUSE 2027 list, Clery hazing ASR timing.

**Implication for Last Copper.** STRENGTHENS the premise, but the dossier's rule citations must be updated now. FCC 26-19 (published Apr 20 2026, copper/discontinuance amendments effective Oct 15 2026) did six things: it explicitly refused the alarm industry's request to keep replacement services compatible with life-safety alarm receivers; it noted that the 2016 device-compatibility requirement sunset in 2025; it declined any post-discontinuance remediation duty; it extended the 31-day auto-grant to dominant carriers (Verizon NJ); it cut notice to 15 days for idle copper; and it preempts conflicting state rules. That is a dated, citable, post-cutoff federal statement that nobody is required to find or prove life-safety lines. It also hands Verizon a motive: the FCC 'encourage[s] carriers to ensure their IP networks are appropriately configured so as to prevent alarm signaling failures', which is PROVE's job. Fix the slide line '63.71(f)(2) auto-grants on day 31': under the new rules all discontinuances get the 31-day auto-grant with a 15-day objection window. Also add that 51.333 now requires direct notice to 911 service providers. Risks: (1) the FCC says device providers 'have been on notice for almost a decade', so a judge may argue the elevator and alarm vendors own this problem, not Verizon. Answer with Cutover Watch as Verizon's own risk signal. (2) Skylo NTN backup modules (Blues, Particle) need sky view, so they will not fix indoor machine rooms; use them only for outdoor blue-light phones on the roadmap. (3) The Sep 8 NJ notice was issued under the old rule, so the Dec 10 date still stands. Nothing found kills Last Copper.

**Idea seeds:**
- ADA Title II deadline is now Apr 26 2027, seven weeks after Finals (Mar 4 2027), and DOJ says generative AI cannot reliably remediate STEM content. Rutgers has a hard dated mandate the crowd will misdate as April 2026. The Verizon angle is weak unless it is tied to Verizon Business delivery.
- Kali365 device-code phishing (May 2026) and OAuth consent phishing (Sep 2026) bypass Duo-style MFA. Idea: confirm device-code or app-consent approvals with carrier Number Verification (Aduna US launch Jul 8 2026), so approval is bound to the SIM in the student's hand.
- International-student window: fixed admission periods from Sep 15 2026 mean extension-of-stay filings, while the FBI (Sep 17 2026) names international students as targets of fake law-enforcement calls ($1.6B, Jan 2025 to Jul 2026). A dated, specific population at a specific moment.
- After the ShinyHunters LMS breach, the FBI warned of follow-on extortion calls and texts to students' families plus swatting. Idea: a post-breach 'quarantine' playbook using carrier signals for the affected roster.
- Comment windows students can file in before Round 2: FTC impersonation-platform ANPRM (due Nov 30 2026), FCC unlicensed D2D NPRM (Nov 9 2026), FCC Robocall Mitigation Database NPRM (replies Nov 9 2026). A filed comment backed by Rutgers primary data is a dated, unique artifact.
- Verizon bundled Google AI Pro to subscribers on Oct 14 2026. On Oct 15 2026 Rutgers OIT told staff not to use free or discounted AI for Rutgers work. Idea: a carrier-delivered, policy-compliant 'campus-safe AI perk' (Verizon AI Skills for America already lists Anthropic and OpenAI as partners).
- CMMC Phase 2 (about Nov 10 2026) requires C3PAO Level 2 for DoD contracts handling CUI. Idea: a Verizon Business managed 'CUI lab enclave' for Rutgers research labs (E&PS revenue; not in the crowd list).
- RUWireless shutoff in early Jan 2027 forces every legacy device onto eduroam or RUatHome. Students can measure which campus devices (IoT, labs, accessibility devices) break, which is primary data.
- Copper theft and vandalism: Verizon cites 6,297 network attacks in California in 2025. Retiring copper removes a theft target, which is a Verizon cost-avoidance line for Last Copper's CFO slide (California data, not NJ).
- 6 GHz GVP (effective Apr 27 2026) allows higher-power outdoor unlicensed Wi-Fi with geofencing. Possible campus outdoor coverage at bus stops or SHI, but it is not Verizon-native.
